    54	impl Runner<'_> {
    55	    // @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — no predicted delta enters receipt-derived state.
    56	    pub(super) fn defer_receipt_maintain(&self, m: &Maintain) -> bool {
    57	        self.device && self.schema.receipt_dependencies.contains_key(&m.target)
    58	    }
    59	
    60	    // Cache ordinary group keys once per target/query. Predicates and receipt
    61	    // fields are never evaluated; unknown/computed keys conservatively freeze
    62	    // the target. An unpinned read is incomplete if any group is dirty, while
    63	    // each returned row still decides independently whether to recount.
    64	    #[inline(never)]
    65	    pub(super) fn receipt_maintain_pending(&mut self, m: &Maintain, group: Option<&[Value]>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    66	        if !self.defer_receipt_maintain(m) { return Ok(false); }
    67	        if !self.receipt_pending_groups.contains_key(&m.target) {
    68	            let fields = if let Some(k) = &m.row_key { vec![k.source_key.clone()] }
    69	                else { m.by.iter().map(|f| m.aliases.get(f).unwrap_or(f).clone()).collect() };
    70	            let mut groups = self.receipt_overlay_groups(&m.source, &fields, !m.computed.is_empty())?;
    71	            if let Some(k) = &m.row_key {
    72	                let member = self.receipt_overlay_groups(&k.table, std::slice::from_ref(&k.member_key), false)?;
    73	                groups = groups.zip(member).map(|(mut source, member)| { source.extend(member); source });
    74	            }
    75	            self.receipt_pending_groups.insert(m.target.clone(), groups);
    76	        }
    77	        let dirty = self.receipt_pending_groups[&m.target].as_ref().is_none_or(|keys|
    78	            group.map_or(!keys.is_empty(), |group| keys.iter().any(|key| key == group)));
    79	        if dirty {
    80	            self.value_source_incomplete = true;
    81	            self.components.note(|frame| frame.pending = true);
    82	        }
    83	        Ok(dirty)
    84	    }
    85	
    86	    fn receipt_overlay_groups(&self, table: &str, fields: &[String], computed: bool) -> Result<Option<Vec<Vec<Value>>>, RuntimeError> {
    87	        if !self.tx.has_provisional(table)? { return Ok(Some(Vec::new())); }
    88	        if computed || fields.iter().any(|f| self.schema.receipt_dependencies.get(table).is_some_and(|owned| owned.contains(f))) {
    89	            return Ok(None);
    90	        }
    91	        self.tx.provisional_groups(table, fields)
    92	    }
    93	
    94	    // Recover a read's parent/group from the pinned index. A member point uses
    95	    // one ordinary-key lookup; a scan without the parent stays conservative.
    96	    pub(super) fn receipt_read_group(&mut self, m: &Maintain, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<Vec<Value>>, RuntimeError> {
    97	        if !self.defer_receipt_maintain(m) { return Ok(None); }
    98	        let columns = self.schema.tables[&m.target].indexes[index].columns();
    99	        let pinned = |field: &str| columns.iter().position(|c| c == field).and_then(|i| key.get(i)).cloned();
   100	        if let Some(k) = &m.row_key {
   101	            if let Some(parent) = pinned(&k.member_key) { return Ok(Some(vec![parent])); }
   102	            if let Some(id) = pinned("member").or_else(|| pinned("id")) {
   103	                let row = self.metered_scan(|r, scope| r.tx.get(scope, &k.table, id.as_str()?))?;
   104	                return Ok(row.and_then(|row| row.field(&k.member_key).ok().cloned()).map(|v| vec![v]));
   105	            }
   106	            return Ok(None);
   107	        }
   108	        Ok(m.by.iter().map(|f| pinned(f)).collect())
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    pub(super) fn maintain(&mut self, source: &str, old: Option<&Value>, new: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   112	        self.receipt_pending_groups.clear();
   113	        // Inserts ordinarily charge their actual aggregate work. A deferred
   114	        // predicate cannot select that work on a device, so use the same public
   115	        // worst-case reservation as update/delete before skipping it. Already
   116	        // prepaid writes must not pay twice.
   117	        if self.device && !self.maintain_prepaid && self.schema.maintains.iter().any(|m|
   118	            m.source == source && m.private.is_none() && m.row_key.is_none() && (self.schema.receipt_dependencies.contains_key(&m.target) || self.schema.tables[&m.target].sync.is_none())) {
   119	            self.reserve_maintains(source, &self.schema.tables[source])?;
   120	        }
   121	        self.maintain_row_views(source, old, new)?;
   122	        let maintains = self.schema.maintains.iter().filter(|maintain| maintain.source == source && maintain.row_key.is_none());
   123	        for maintain in maintains {
   124	            if self.defer_receipt_maintain(maintain) || (self.device && self.schema.tables[&maintain.target].sync.is_none()) { continue; }
   125	            let source_bytes = super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[&maintain.source]);
   126	            let target = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.target];
   127	            let bytes = source_bytes.saturating_mul(2).saturating_add(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(target).saturating_mul(16 + 4 * target.indexes.len() as u64));
   128	            let prepaid = self.maintain_prepaid;
   129	            if maintain.private.is_some() && self.column_prepaid { self.maintain_prepaid = true; }
   130	            let result = self.maintain_scratch(bytes, |runner| runner.maintain_one(maintain, old, new));
   131	            self.maintain_prepaid = prepaid;
   132	            result?;
   133	        }
   134	        Ok(())
   135	    }
   136	
   137	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — reserve interpreted images, both keys, targets and index buffers before allocation; release transient ownership on every exit.
   138	    pub(super) fn maintain_scratch<T>(&mut self, bytes: u64, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
   139	        if self.maintain_prepaid { return work(self); }
   140	        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
   141	        let result = work(self);
   142	        self.meter.used.heap_bytes = self.meter.used.heap_bytes.saturating_sub(bytes);
   143	        result
   144	    }
   145	
   146	    fn maintain_one(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, old: Option<&Value>, new: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   147	        let table = self.schema.table(&maintain.source).expect("validated");
   148	        let mut changes: Vec<(Vec<Value>, i128)> = Vec::new();
   149	        for (image, direction) in [(old, -1), (new, 1)] {
   150	            let Some(image) = image else { continue };
   151	            self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Steps, maintain.evaluation_steps())?;
   152	            let image = crate::schema::row_image(image, table);
   153	            if !maintain.selected(&image) { continue; }
   154	            let key = maintain.source_key(&image);
   155	            let change = delta(maintain, &image, direction)?;
   156	            if let Some(existing) = changes.iter_mut().find(|(k, _)| *k == key) { existing.1 += change; }
   157	            else { changes.push((key, change)); }
   158	        }
   159	        for (key, change) in changes {
   160	            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   161	            if let Some(retiring) = self.retiring.as_mut() {
   162	                let entry = retiring.aggregates.entry((maintain.target.clone(), canonical_json(&key))).or_insert_with(|| (key, 0));
   163	                entry.1 = entry.1.checked_add(change).ok_or_else(|| aggregate_overflow(maintain))?;
   164	                continue;
   165	            }
   166	            if change != 0 || self.live_sum(maintain) {
   167	                self.apply_aggregate(maintain, key, change, None)?;
   168	            }
   169	        }
   170	        Ok(())
   171	    }
   172	
   173	    /// Apply a retirement's net aggregate changes, one per target row, in
   174	    /// key order. Only a total the commit really ends with can overflow.
   175	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   176	    pub(super) fn apply_retired_aggregates(&mut self, changes: BTreeMap<(String, String), (Vec<Value>, i128)>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   177	        let schema = self.schema;
   178	        for ((target, _), (key, change)) in changes {
   179	            let maintain = schema.maintain_for(&target).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing maintain for {target}")))?;
   180	            if change != 0 || self.live_sum(maintain) {
   181	                self.apply_aggregate(maintain, key, change, None)?;
   182	            }
   183	        }
   184	        Ok(())
   185	    }

   187	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   188	    pub(crate) fn rebuild_maintain_reported(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, report: &mut crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   189	        let started = std::time::Instant::now();
   190	        self.rebuilt_source_rows = 0;
   191	        self.rebuild_maintain(maintain)?;
   192	        let rows = self.rebuilt_source_rows;
   193	        report.views.push(crate::store::adoption_report::ViewBuild { name: maintain.label(), rows, elapsed: started.elapsed() });
   194	        Ok(())
   195	    }

   197	    /// Derive every legacy-to-digest aggregate id map to a fixed point, then
   198	    /// rewrite typed references before rebuilding the target rows. The fixed
   199	    /// point matters when one aggregate groups by a reference to another.
   200	    /// Every pass is inside the adoption writer transaction, so no reader can
   201	    /// observe a dangling intermediate reference.
   202	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   203	    pub(crate) fn rewrite_legacy_maintained_references(&mut self, maintains: &[Maintain]) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   204	        let mut legacy = Vec::new();
   205	        for maintain in maintains.iter().filter(|maintain| maintain.private.is_none() && maintain.row_key.is_none()) {
   206	            let target = self.schema.tables[&maintain.target].clone();
   207	            let fields: Vec<_> = maintain.group_fields().into_iter().map(|field| {
   208	                let kind = target.columns.get(&field).expect("validated maintain group").clone();
   209	                (field, kind)
   210	            }).collect();
   211	            let mut rows = Vec::new();
   212	            let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
   213	            loop {
   214	                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, &maintain.target, "byId", &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, 256))?;
   215	                if page.is_empty() { break; }
   216	                for (row, encoded) in page {
   217	                    bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&encoded);
   218	                    let old = row.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
   219	                    let key = fields.iter().map(|(field, kind)| Ok((kind.clone(), row.field(field)?.clone())))
   220	                        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, RuntimeError>>()?;
   221	                    rows.push((old, key));
   222	                }
   223	            }
   224	            legacy.push((maintain.target.clone(), rows));
   225	        }
   226	
   227	        let mut ids: BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>> = BTreeMap::new();
   228	        let mut settled = false;
   229	        for _ in 0..=legacy.len() {
   230	            let mut next_ids: BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>> = BTreeMap::new();
   231	            for (target, rows) in &legacy {
   232	                let target_ids = next_ids.entry(target.clone()).or_default();
   233	                for (old, key) in rows {
   234	                    let key: Vec<_> = key.iter().map(|(kind, value)| {
   235	                        let mut value = value.clone();
   236	                        rewrite_legacy_maintained_reference(kind, &mut value, &ids);
   237	                        value
   238	                    }).collect();
   239	                    let next = maintained_id(&key);
   240	                    if old != &next { target_ids.insert(old.clone(), next); }
   241	                }
   242	            }
   243	            if next_ids == ids {
   244	                ids = next_ids;
   245	                settled = true;
   246	                break;
   247	            }
   248	            ids = next_ids;
   249	        }
   250	        if !settled {
   251	            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema("legacy aggregate identity references form a cycle".into()));
   252	        }
   253	
   254	        let references: Vec<_> = self.schema.tables.iter().filter_map(|(name, table)| {
   255	            let columns: Vec<_> = table.columns.iter().filter_map(|(column, kind)|
   256	                has_legacy_maintained_reference(kind, &ids).then(|| (column.clone(), kind.clone())))
   257	                .collect();
   258	            (!columns.is_empty()).then(|| (name.clone(), table.clone(), columns))
   259	        }).collect();
   260	        let mut rewritten = 0_u64;
   261	        for (name, table, columns) in references {
   262	            let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
   263	            loop {
   264	                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, &name, "byId", &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, 256))?;
   265	                if page.is_empty() { break; }
   266	                for (old, encoded) in page {
   267	                    bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&encoded);
   268	                    let mut next = old.clone();
   269	                    let mut changed = false;
   270	                    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut next {
   271	                        for (column, kind) in &columns {
   272	                            let Some(value) = fields.get_mut(column) else { continue };
   273	                            changed |= rewrite_legacy_maintained_reference(kind, value, &ids);
   274	                        }
   275	                    }
   276	                    if changed {
   277	                        self.tx.put(&name, &table, &next, Some(&old))?;
   278	                        rewritten = rewritten.saturating_add(1);
   279	                    }
   280	                }
   281	            }
   282	        }
   283	        Ok(rewritten)
   284	    }

   286	    /// Build one aggregate from the rows in the store: the target's rows go,
   287	    /// then every source row contributes once. A schema change that adds or
   288	    /// redefines a `maintain` calls this, so a count over existing rows is
   289	    /// right from its first read (day two, LLP 2000.000 §11.2).
   290	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   291	    pub(crate) fn rebuild_maintain(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   292	        let heap = self.meter.used.heap_bytes;
   293	        let saved = self.column_rebuild;
   294	        self.column_rebuild |= maintain.private.is_some();
   295	        let result = self.rebuild_maintain_inner(maintain);
   296	        self.column_rebuild = saved;
   297	        self.meter.used.heap_bytes = heap;
   298	        result
   299	    }
   300	
   301	    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
   302	    fn rebuild_maintain_inner(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   303	        if maintain.row_key.is_some() { return self.rebuild_row_view(maintain); }
   304	        let target = self.schema.table(&maintain.target).expect("validated");
   305	        let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
   306	        let target_bytes = super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(target).saturating_mul(16 + 4 * target.indexes.len() as u64);
   307	        self.meter.heap(target_bytes.saturating_mul(256))?;
   308	        loop {
   309	            let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, &maintain.target, "byId", &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, 256))?;
   310	            if page.is_empty() { break; }
   311	            for (existing, key) in page {
   312	                bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&key);
   313	                self.refuse_timeline_write(&maintain.target, crate::schema::Effect::Delete)?;
   314	                let entries = self.tx.delete(&maintain.target, target, &existing)?;
   315	                self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::IndexEntries, entries)?;
   316	                self.row_changed(&maintain.target, Some(&existing), None, Origin::Maintained)?;
   317	            }
   318	        }
   319	        let source = self.schema.table(&maintain.source).expect("validated");
   320	        self.meter.heap(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(source).saturating_mul(256))?;
   321	        let mut totals: BTreeMap<String, (Vec<Value>, i128)> = BTreeMap::new();
   322	        bounds = Bounds::default();
   323	        loop {
   324	            let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, &maintain.source, "byId", &[], &bounds, Dir::Asc, 256))?;
   325	            if page.is_empty() { break; }
   326	            for (mut image, key) in page {
   327	                self.rebuilt_source_rows += 1;
   328	                bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&key);
   329	                materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, source);
   330	                self.charge_maintain(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;

   333	                // @ref LLP 3000.028#6 — a total unique owner key has one contribution; backfill streams bounded pages instead of retaining all lifetime keys in a totals map.
   334	                if maintain.own_keys.is_some() {
   335	                    let heap = self.meter.used.heap_bytes;
   336	                    self.meter.heap(target_bytes)?;
   337	                    self.apply_aggregate(maintain, maintain.source_key(&image), 1, Some(true))?;
   338	                    self.meter.used.heap_bytes = heap;
   339	                    continue;
   340	                }
   341	                self.meter.heap(target_bytes)?;
   342	                let key = maintain.source_key(&image);
   343	                let change = delta(maintain, &image, 1)?;
   344	                let entry = match totals.entry(canonical_json(&key)) {
   345	                    std::collections::btree_map::Entry::Occupied(entry) => {

   337	fn email_format_is_one_shared_ascii_contract() {
   338	    let source = r#"
   339	table contacts:
   340	  email: text 3..254 format email

   343	  online only
   344	mutation addUnchecked(id: id, email: text 3..254):
   345	  return insert contacts { id, email }
   346	query echo(email: text 3..254 format email):
   347	  return email
   348	"#;
   349	    let mut engine = engine(source);
   350	    for email in ["a@example.com", "first.last+tag@sub.example", "x@localhost"] {
   351	        let result = engine.query("echo", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("email".into(), Value::String(email.into()))]))).unwrap();
   352	        assert_eq!(result.data, Value::String(email.into()));
   353	    }
   354	    for email in ["a..b@example.com", ".a@example.com", "a@-example.com", "a@example..com", "a@пример.рф", "a@@example.com"] {
   355	        let error = engine.query("echo", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("email".into(), Value::String(email.into()))]))).unwrap_err();
   356	        assert_eq!(error.code, "E_INPUT", "{email}: {error:?}");
   357	    }
   358	    let rejected = engine.mutate("addUnchecked", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
   359	        ("id".into(), Value::Id("bad".into())), ("email".into(), Value::String("a..b@example.com".into())),
   360	    ]))).unwrap();
   361	    assert!(matches!(rejected.state, WriteState::Failed { ref why, .. } if why.code == "E_CONSTRAINT"), "{rejected:?}");
   362	}

   388	#[test]
   389	fn rounded_division_is_exact_signed_and_keeps_numeric_types() {
   390	    let source = r#"
   391	query rounded():
   392	  return [
   393	    divide(5, 2, 'toward-zero'), divide(5, 2, 'away-from-zero'),
   394	    divide(5, 2, 'floor'), divide(5, 2, 'ceiling'),
   395	    divide(5, 2, 'half-away-from-zero'), divide(5, 2, 'half-even'),
   396	    divide(7, 2, 'half-even'), divide(-5, 2, 'half-away-from-zero'),
   397	    divide(-5, 2, 'floor'), divide(-5, 2, 'ceiling'),
   398	    divide(5, -2, 'floor'), divide(5, -2, 'ceiling'),
   399	    divide(7, 3, 'half-even'), divide(8, 3, 'half-even')
   400	  ]
   401	query decimalValue(value: decimal 2):
   402	  return divide(value, 2, 'half-even')
   403	query moneyValue(value: money USD 2):
   404	  return divide(value, 2, 'half-even')
   405	query scaled(numerator: int, denominator: int):
   406	  return [
   407	    divide(numerator, denominator, 2, 'half-even'),
   408	    divide(numerator, denominator, 2, 'half-away-from-zero')
   409	  ]
   410	query wide(numerator: int, denominator: int):
   411	  return divide(numerator, denominator, 12, 'half-even')
   412	query scaleZero(numerator: int, denominator: int):
   413	  return divide(numerator, denominator, 0, 'half-even')
   414	query scaledOverflow():
   415	  return divide(9007199254740991, 1, 1, 'half-even')
   416	query zero():
   417	  return divide(1, 0, 'half-even')
   418	"#;
   419	    let bound = checked(source);
   420	    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "decimalValue").unwrap().result, Ty::Decimal(2));
   421	    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "moneyValue").unwrap().result, Ty::Money("USD".into(), 2));
   422	    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "scaled").unwrap().result,
   423	        Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::Decimal(2)), 2));
   424	    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "wide").unwrap().result, Ty::Decimal(12));
   425	    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "scaleZero").unwrap().result, Ty::Decimal(0));
   426	    let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
   427	    let values = engine.query("rounded", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
   428	    assert_eq!(values, Value::Array([2, 3, 2, 3, 3, 2, 4, -3, -3, -2, -3, -2, 2, 3].into_iter().map(Value::Int).collect()));
   429	    for name in ["decimalValue", "moneyValue"] {
   430	        let value = engine.query(name, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("value".into(), Value::Int(5))]))).unwrap().data;
   431	        assert_eq!(value, Value::Int(2), "{name}");
   432	    }
   433	    let scaled = |engine: &mut Engine, numerator, denominator| engine.query("scaled", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
   434	        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(numerator)), ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(denominator)),
   435	    ]))).unwrap().data;
   436	    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, 101, 2), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(5050), Value::Int(5050)]));
   437	    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, -101, 2), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(-5050), Value::Int(-5050)]));
   438	    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, 1, 8), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(12), Value::Int(13)]));
   439	    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, -1, 8), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(-12), Value::Int(-13)]));
   440	    assert_eq!(engine.query("wide", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
   441	        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX)),
   442	        ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX)),
   443	    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(1_000_000_000_000), "the wide scaled intermediate is not restricted to i53");
   444	    assert_eq!(engine.query("scaleZero", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
   445	        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(5)), ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(2)),
   446	    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(2));
   447	    assert_eq!(engine.query("scaledOverflow", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_OVERFLOW");
   448	    assert_eq!(engine.query("zero", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_DIV_ZERO");
   449	
   450	    use snapback4_core::ir::{Builtin, Expr, Program, ProgramKind, Rounding, Stmt};
   451	    let overflow = Program {
   452	        name: "overflow".into(), kind: ProgramKind::Query, args: BTreeMap::new(),
   453	        read_time_granularity_ms: None, native: false, returned_shapes: BTreeMap::new(),
   454	        body: vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::Divide { rounding: Rounding::TowardZero, scale: None },
   455	            args: vec![Expr::Literal(Value::Int(i64::MIN)), Expr::Literal(Value::Int(-1))] })],
   456	    };
   457	    let mut raw = Engine::memory(Default::default(), [overflow]).unwrap();
   458	    assert_eq!(raw.query("overflow", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_OVERFLOW");
   459	
   460	    for bad in ["divide(1, 2, 'nearest')", "divide(1, 2, mode)", "divide(1, divisor, 'half-even')",
   461	        "divide(1, 2, 13, 'half-even')", "divide(1, 2, scale, 'half-even')"] {
   462	        let source = if bad.contains("mode") { format!("query bad(mode: enum('half-even')):\n  return {bad}\n") }
   463	            else if bad.contains("scale") { format!("query bad(scale: int):\n  return {bad}\n") }
   464	            else if bad.contains("divisor") { format!("query bad(divisor: decimal 2):\n  return {bad}\n") }
   465	            else { format!("query bad():\n  return {bad}\n") };
   466	        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), source)]);
   467	        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"), "{bad}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
   468	    }
   469	}

     1	/-!
     2	# Wave 12 semantic witnesses
     3	
     4	These executable definitions cover language contracts outside the row IR:
     5	global count visibility, capture-time count provenance, email validation, and
     6	enum values entering the existing word-search path. Rounded division lives in
     7	`Quarry.Eval` because it is an IR builtin.
     8	-/
     9	
    10	namespace Quarry.Wave12
    11	
    12	def globalCount (rows : List α) : Nat := rows.length
    13	
    14	@[simp] theorem globalCount_empty : globalCount ([] : List α) = 0 := rfl
    15	
    16	theorem positive_count_has_member {rows : List α} (h : 0 < globalCount rows) :
    17	    ∃ row, row ∈ rows := by
    18	  cases rows with
    19	  | nil => simp [globalCount] at h
    20	  | cons row rows => exact ⟨row, by simp⟩
    21	
    22	/-- A readable absent global count is zero; hidden hit and miss are both
    23	`none`, so absence never becomes a disclosure channel. -/
    24	def visibleGlobalCount (authorized : Bool) (stored : Option Nat) : Option Nat :=
    25	  if authorized then some (stored.getD 0) else none
    26	
    27	example : visibleGlobalCount true none = some 0 := rfl
    28	example : visibleGlobalCount false none = none := rfl
    29	example : visibleGlobalCount false (some 7) = none := rfl
    30	
    31	/-- Binder provenance is a capture-time domain, transaction and authority
    32	triple. A retained diagnostic origin is intentionally not part of it. -/
    33	structure CountCapture where
    34	  domain : String
    35	  transaction : Nat
    36	  authority : String
    37	  definition : Nat
    38	  deriving DecidableEq
    39	
    40	def countCaptureLive (capture : CountCapture) (domain : String)
    41	    (transaction : Nat) (authority : String) (definition : Nat) : Bool :=
    42	  capture.domain == domain && capture.transaction == transaction &&
    43	    capture.authority == authority && capture.definition == definition

    47	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    48	    "entries/owner=alice" 5 "alice" 7 = false := rfl
    49	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    50	    "entries/owner=bob" 4 "alice" 7 = false := rfl
    51	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    52	    "entries/owner=alice" 4 "bob" 7 = false := rfl
    53	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    54	    "entries/owner=alice" 4 "alice" 8 = false := rfl
    55	
    56	/-- A keyed maintained count becomes required only for the exact key whose
    57	role predicate was proved in the same transaction. A readable miss is zero;
    58	hidden hits and misses share `none`. -/
    59	def keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard (guardKey readKey : String)
    60	    (sameTransaction authorized : Bool) (stored : Option Nat) : Option Nat :=
    61	  if sameTransaction && authorized && guardKey == readKey
    62	    then some (stored.getD 0)
    63	    else none
    64	
    65	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-a" true true none = some 0 := rfl
    66	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-b" true true (some 7) = none := rfl
    67	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-a" false true (some 7) = none := rfl
    68	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-a" true false (some 7) = none := rfl
    69	
    70	def asciiAlphaNum (c : Char) : Bool :=
    71	  let n := c.toNat
    72	  (65 ≤ n && n ≤ 90) || (97 ≤ n && n ≤ 122) || (48 ≤ n && n ≤ 57)
    73	
    74	def asciiLower (c : Char) : Char :=
    75	  if 65 ≤ c.toNat && c.toNat ≤ 90 then Char.ofNat (c.toNat + 32) else c
    76	
    77	def splitOnce (needle : Char) : List Char → Option (List Char × List Char)
    78	  | [] => none
    79	  | c :: cs =>
    80	      if c == needle then some ([], cs) else
    81	        (splitOnce needle cs).map fun pair => (c :: pair.1, pair.2)
    82	
    83	def splitDots : List Char → List (List Char)
    84	  | [] => [[]]
    85	  | c :: cs =>
    86	      let rest := splitDots cs
    87	      if c == '.' then [] :: rest else
    88	        match rest with
    89	        | [] => [[c]]
    90	        | label :: labels => (c :: label) :: labels
    91	
    92	def noAdjacentDots : List Char → Bool
    93	  | '.' :: '.' :: _ => false
    94	  | _ :: cs => noAdjacentDots cs
    95	  | [] => true
    96	
    97	def localPunctuation : List Char :=
    98	  ['.', '!', '#', '$', '%', '&', '\'', '*', '+', '/', '=', '?', '^', '_', '`', '{', '|', '}', '~', '-']
    99	
   100	def localPartValid (part : List Char) : Bool :=
   101	  !part.isEmpty && part.length ≤ 64 && part.head? != some '.' &&
   102	    part.getLast? != some '.' && noAdjacentDots part &&
   103	    part.all fun c => asciiAlphaNum c || localPunctuation.contains c
   104	
   105	def domainLabelValid (label : List Char) : Bool :=
   106	  !label.isEmpty && label.length ≤ 63 &&
   107	    label.head?.any asciiAlphaNum && label.getLast?.any asciiAlphaNum &&
   108	    label.all fun c => asciiAlphaNum c || c == '-'
   109	
   110	def emailValid (email : String) : Bool :=
   111	  let chars := email.toList
   112	  chars.length ≤ 254 && chars.all (·.toNat ≤ 127) &&
   113	    chars.count '@' == 1 && match splitOnce '@' chars with
   114	      | some (localChars, domain) => localPartValid localChars &&
   115	          !domain.isEmpty && domain.length ≤ 253 && (splitDots domain).all domainLabelValid
   116	      | none => false
   117	
   118	example : emailValid "first.last+tag@sub.example" = true := by decide
   119	example : emailValid "x@localhost" = true := by decide
   120	example : emailValid "a..b@example.com" = false := by decide
   121	example : emailValid "a@-example.com" = false := by decide
   122	example : emailValid "a@пример.рф" = false := by decide
   123	
   124	/-- ASCII executable witness for enum spellings using the shared word-search
   125	shape: lowercase, alphanumeric token boundaries, and query-prefix matching.
   126	The Rust/device tests own NFC and the production Unicode tables. -/
   127	def asciiTokens (text : String) : List (List Char) :=
   128	  let rec go (remaining current : List Char) (tokens : List (List Char)) :=
   129	    match remaining with
   130	    | [] => (if current.isEmpty then tokens else current.reverse :: tokens).reverse
   131	    | c :: cs =>
   132	        if asciiAlphaNum c then go cs (asciiLower c :: current) tokens
   133	        else go cs [] (if current.isEmpty then tokens else current.reverse :: tokens)
   134	  go text.toList [] []
   135	
   136	def isPrefix : List Char → List Char → Bool
   137	  | [], _ => true
   138	  | _, [] => false
   139	  | p :: ps, w :: ws => p == w && isPrefix ps ws
   140	
   141	def enumMatchesAscii (value query : String) : Bool :=
   142	  let words := asciiTokens value
   143	  (asciiTokens query).all fun term => words.any fun word => isPrefix term word
   144	
   145	example : enumMatchesAscii "coffee-shop" "cof" = true := by decide
   146	example : enumMatchesAscii "book_store" "BOOK" = true := by decide
   147	example : enumMatchesAscii "museum" "music" = false := by decide
   148	
   149	end Quarry.Wave12

+/// Exact rounded division comes from its own stream, preserving every older
+/// case whose stream does not select the new expression.
+fn rounded_division(case: &mut Case) {
+    let mut rng = Rng::new(case.seed ^ ROUND_DIV_STREAM);
+    if rng.pick(6) != 0 || !matches!(case.program.body, Body::Scalar(_)) { return; }
+    let modes = [Rounding::TowardZero, Rounding::AwayFromZero, Rounding::Floor,
+        Rounding::Ceiling, Rounding::HalfAwayFromZero, Rounding::HalfEven];
+    let dividend = if rng.pick(2) == 0 { Expr::Arg("a".into(), Type::Int) }
+        else { Expr::Int([-7, -5, -1, 0, 1, 5, 7][rng.pick(7) as usize]) };
+    let divisor = if rng.pick(2) == 0 { Expr::Arg("b".into(), Type::Int) }
+        else { Expr::Int([-3, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3][rng.pick(7) as usize]) };
+    let scale = match rng.pick(5) { 0 => None, 1 => Some(0), 2 => Some(2), 3 => Some(6), _ => Some(12) };
+    case.program.body = Body::Scalar(Expr::RoundedDivide(
+        modes[rng.pick(modes.len() as u64) as usize].clone(), scale, Box::new(dividend), Box::new(divisor)));

+/-- Truncating quotient/remainder followed by one exact rounding step. The
+model deliberately derives truncation from the existing Euclidean primitive,
+so its oracle is independent of the Rust implementation. -/
+def roundedDiv (rounding : Rounding) (scale : Option Nat) (x y : Int) : EM Value := do
+  if y = 0 then refuse "E_DIV_ZERO"
+  let x := x * (10 : Int) ^ scale.getD 0
+  let qe := Int.ediv x y
+  let re := Int.emod x y
+  let q := if x < 0 ∧ re ≠ 0 then qe + (if y < 0 then -1 else 1) else qe
+  let r := x - q * y
+  if r = 0 then checkI53 q else
+    let direction : Int := if (x < 0) = (y < 0) then 1 else -1
+    let away := q + direction
+    let result := match rounding with
+      | .towardZero => q
+      | .awayFromZero => away
+      | .floor => if direction < 0 then away else q
+      | .ceiling => if 0 < direction then away else q
+      | .halfAwayFromZero => if Int.natAbs r * 2 ≥ Int.natAbs y then away else q
+      | .halfEven =>
+          if Int.natAbs r * 2 > Int.natAbs y ∨
+              (Int.natAbs r * 2 = Int.natAbs y ∧ Int.emod q 2 ≠ 0)
+          then away else q
+    checkI53 result

     4	/-!
     5	# The big-step evaluator
     6	
     7	`evalExpr` and `execStmts` are total functions (structural on a fuel
     8	argument; a checked program never exhausts the default fuel, and
     9	exhaustion is the model-only refusal `E_MODEL_FUEL`). Determinism is
    10	therefore by construction; `Determinism.lean` proves the non-trivial part.
    11	
    12	Reads see only a `View` (the visible rows of each table), never the store.
    13	The statement executor is generic in its *world* `W`: a query runs over a
    14	`View` and refuses every write (`E_QUARRY_PLACEMENT`); a mutation runs over a
    15	`Store` and recomputes the view after each write (`Run.lean`).
    16	
    17	Refusal codes are the engine's where the documents name them (`E_OVERFLOW`,
    18	`E_DIV_ZERO`, `E_BOUND`, `NOT_FOUND`, `E_RULE`, `E_CONFLICT`,
    19	`E_CONSTRAINT`, application codes). `E_MODEL_TYPE` and `E_MODEL_FUEL` mark
    20	states a checked program cannot reach; the harness must treat them as a
    21	model or emitter defect, never as agreement.
    22	-/
    23	
    24	namespace Quarry
    25	
    26	/-- The meter is out of scope (LLP 3000.035 §5.2) except one abstract
    27	ceiling: the number of *visible* candidates a request may examine
    28	(OPEN M1). `none` is unbounded. -/
    29	structure Ctx where
    30	  schema : Schema
    31	  viewer : List Nat
    32	  /-- The server request clock used by lower read lifetimes. -/
    33	  now : Int := 0
    34	  helpers : List Helper := []
    35	  budget : Option Nat := none
    36	  mutation : Bool := false
    37	  /-- Evaluating a helper's body: the engine never plans a helper's page as a
    38	  merge of its branches (LLP 3000.036.001 §2), so neither does the model. -/
    39	  inHelper : Bool := false
    40	
    41	/-- Request state: visible candidates examined so far, and whether every
    42	read so far was complete. -/
    43	structure RS where
    44	  used : Nat := 0
    45	  complete : Bool := true
    46	  deriving Repr, DecidableEq
    47	
    48	inductive Stop where
    49	  | refuse (code : String)
    50	  /-- A query whose scalar read was cut: no value, no continuation
    51	  (bounds card: "a streaming query total with a meter cut … `loading`"). -/
    52	  | unavailable
    53	  deriving Repr, DecidableEq
    54	
    55	abbrev EM := StateT RS (Except Stop)
    56	
    57	/-- A local's value and, if it was bound directly to a page, that page's
    58	continuation. -/
    59	abbrev Env := List (String × (Value × Option Value))
    60	
    61	def refuse {α : Type} (code : String) : EM α := throw (.refuse code)
    62	def typeErr {α : Type} : EM α := refuse "E_MODEL_TYPE"
    63	
    64	def checkI53 (n : Int) : EM Value :=
    65	  if inI53 n then pure (.ofInt n) else refuse "E_OVERFLOW"
    66	
    67	def ordered (op : BinOp) (o : Ordering) : Bool :=
    68	  match op with
    69	  | .lt => o == .lt
    70	  | .le => o != .gt
    71	  | .gt => o == .gt
    72	  | .ge => o != .lt
    73	  | _ => false
    74	
    75	/-- Binary operators on evaluated operands. Integers are i53 and refuse
    76	overflow; `/` and `%` are Euclidean (language card: "the same Euclidean
    77	parts as divmod") and refuse a zero divisor. `+` also concatenates text and
    78	lists. Ordered comparison needs two non-null scalars of one kind. -/
    79	def binop (op : BinOp) (a b : Value) : EM Value :=
    80	  match op, a, b with
    81	  | .add, .s (.int x), .s (.int y) => checkI53 (x + y)
    82	  | .add, .s (.text x), .s (.text y) => pure (.s (.text (x ++ y)))
    83	  | .add, .arr xs, .arr ys => pure (.arr (xs ++ ys))
    84	  | .sub, .s (.int x), .s (.int y) => checkI53 (x - y)
    85	  | .mul, .s (.int x), .s (.int y) => checkI53 (x * y)
    86	  | .div, .s (.int x), .s (.int y) =>

    88	  | .mod, .s (.int x), .s (.int y) =>

    91	  | .ne, a, b => pure (.ofBool !(a == b))
    92	  | op, .s x, .s y =>
    93	    if x.rank = y.rank && x != .null then pure (.ofBool (ordered op (x.cmp y)))
    94	    else typeErr
    95	  | _, _, _ => typeErr

   121

   125	def calApply : CalFn → Int → Option Int

//! Binding declarations to the core schema: tables, indexes, rules,
//! constraints, maintained aggregates and their generated targets, and the
//! `sync` line with the principal column resolved.

use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
use crate::ast::*;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, Site, ambiguous, name_rewrite, sync_error, type_error, unknown};
use crate::types::{Ty, scalar_to_column};
use snapback4_core::ir::KeyProvenance;
use snapback4_core::schema::{self as core, Audience, Constraint, Horizon, LeavePolicy, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Rules, Sync, Table};
use snapback4_core::value::Value;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

pub fn bind_schema(catalog: &Catalog, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> core::Schema {
    let mut schema = core::Schema::default();
    for info in catalog.tables.values() {
        let table = bind_table(catalog, info, diagnostics);
        for index in &info.indexes {
            if let Some((code, message)) = &index.refusal {
                match super::program::validate_code(&index.site, code) {
                    Ok(()) => {
                        schema.unique_refusals.entry(info.decl.name.clone()).or_default().insert(index.name.clone(), core::UniqueRefusal {
                            code: code.clone(), message: message.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| format!("the program refused with {code}")),
                        });
                    }
                    Err(diagnostic) => diagnostics.push(diagnostic),
                }
            }
        }
        schema.tables.insert(info.decl.name.clone(), table);
    }
    // Identity is semantic, independent of source locations and index planning order.
    for name in schema.through_order() {
        let Some(t) = schema.tables[&name].through.clone() else { continue; };
        let parent=&schema.tables[&t.parent];
        let fields: Vec<_> = t.fields.values().map(|f| (f,parent.columns.get(f),parent.defaults.get(f))).collect();
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — expiry interpretation changes recapture every descendant, even when the rule never reads the deadline.
        let expiry = parent.expire.as_ref().map(|field| (field, parent.columns.get(field), parent.defaults.get(field)));
        let identity=snapback4_core::value::digest(&(&parent.rules.read,fields,expiry,&parent.through,&schema.tables[&name].columns[&t.reference],schema.tables[&name].defaults.get(&t.reference)));
        schema.tables.get_mut(&name).unwrap().through.as_mut().unwrap().identity=identity;

   362	    fn live_sum(&self, maintain: &Maintain) -> bool {

   369	    fn aggregate_source_present(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, key: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {

   377	            let present = runner.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.count_prefix(allowance, &maintain.source, &index, key, 1))? != 0;

   381	                return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("the zero-sum group's source occupancy is not known locally; queued without a predicted row".into()));
   382	            }
   383	            Ok(present)
   384	        })

   387	    fn aggregate_source_complete(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, key: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   388	        let source = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.source];
   389	        if source.sync.as_ref().is_none_or(|sync| sync.horizon.is_some()) || self.schema.maintain_subset_unknown(&maintain.target) { return Ok(false); }

   391	        if !self.upsert_absence_proven(&maintain.target, "byGroup", &target_group_key(maintain, key))? { return Ok(false); }

   393	            let values = index.columns().iter().map_while(|column| maintain.by.iter().position(|field| field == column).map(|n| key[n].clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   394	            if self.retained_group(&maintain.source, name, &values)? { return Ok(false); }
   395	        }
   396	        Ok(true)

   400	        if self.schema.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(&maintain.target)) {

   134	pub(super) fn read_hop_rewrite(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, rule: &Expr) -> Option<String> {

   136	        return Some(rewrite);

   196	/// Two live parents cannot be joined inside a table rule. Name the durable
   197	/// representation that preserves the policy instead of recursively nesting

   199	fn stored_comparison_rewrite(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, rule: &Expr) -> Option<String> {
   200	    fn comparison(expr: &Expr) -> Option<(Vec<String>, Vec<String>)> {
   201	        match expr {
   202	            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" => match (chain(left), chain(right)) {
   203	                (Some(left), Some(right)) => Some((left, right)),
   204	                _ => comparison(left).or_else(|| comparison(right)),
   205	            },
   206	            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => comparison(left).or_else(|| comparison(right)),
   207	            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => comparison(value),
   208	            _ => None,
   209	        }
   210	    }
   211	    let (left, right) = comparison(rule)?;
   212	    if left.len() != 2 || right.len() != 2 || left[0] == right[0] || left[1] != right[1] { return None; }

     1	//! Wave 12 language parity on the web-memory and SQLite device stores.
     2	mod common;
     3	use common::{Host, Memory, Sqlite, compile, id, snapshot_pages, stream_page};
     4	use serde_json::json;
     5	use snapback4_core::{Context, Value, WriteState};
     6	
     7	const SOURCE: &str = r#"
     8	table entries:
     9	  category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum')
    10	  contact: text 3..254 format email
    11	  amount: decimal 2
    12	  search category
    13	  public 'wave 12 device fixture'
    14	  insert <- allow
    15	  sync public
    16	view total: count entries
    17	mutation add(id: id, category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum'), contact: text 3..254, amount: decimal 2):
    18	  return insert entries { id, category, contact, amount }
    19	query totalEntries():
    20	  return total
    21	query search(q: text <=40, c: cursor ?):
    22	  return entries matching q first 20 by .id after c
    23	query extrema():
    24	  n = count(entries)
    25	  require n > 0 else EMPTY
    26	  return { low: min(entries, .amount), high: max(entries, .amount), sum: sum(entries, .amount) }
    27	query rounded(value: decimal 2):
    28	  return [divide(value, 2, 'half-even'), divide(value, 2, 'half-away-from-zero')]
    29	query average(total: int, count: int):
    30	  return divide(total, count, 2, 'half-even')
    31	query email(value: text 3..254 format email):
    32	  return value
    33	"#;
    34	
    35	fn parity<H: Host>(host: &mut H) {
    36	    let (mut server, backend) = compile(SOURCE);
    37	    server.seed([
    38	        ("entries".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
    39	            ("id", id("a")), ("category", Value::String("coffee-shop".into())),
    40	            ("contact", Value::String("a@example.com".into())), ("amount", Value::Int(5)),
    41	        ])),
    42	        ("entries".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
    43	            ("id", id("b")), ("category", Value::String("book_store".into())),
    44	            ("contact", Value::String("b@example.com".into())), ("amount", Value::Int(-5)),
    45	        ])),
    46	    ]).unwrap();
    47	    let mut device = host.open("alice", Some(backend)).unwrap();
    48	    let pages = snapshot_pages(&mut server, "alice", 100);
    49	    for (index, page) in pages.iter().enumerate() { device.apply(page, index == 0).unwrap(); }
    50	
    51	    assert_eq!(device.query("totalEntries", "alice", &json!({}), 10).unwrap().data, Value::Int(2));
    52	    assert_eq!(device.query("extrema", "alice", &json!({}), 10).unwrap().data,
    53	        snapback4_core::value::object([("low", Value::Int(-5)), ("high", Value::Int(5)), ("sum", Value::Int(0))]));
    54	    assert_eq!(device.query("rounded", "alice", &json!({"value":5}), 10).unwrap().data,
    55	        Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(2), Value::Int(3)]));
    56	    assert_eq!(device.query("average", "alice", &json!({"total":101,"count":2}), 10).unwrap().data, Value::Int(5050));
    57	    assert_eq!(device.query("average", "alice", &json!({"total":-1,"count":8}), 10).unwrap().data, Value::Int(-12));
    58	    assert_eq!(device.query("email", "alice", &json!({"value":"a@localhost"}), 10).unwrap().data,
    59	        Value::String("a@localhost".into()));
    60	    assert_eq!(device.query("email", "alice", &json!({"value":"a..b@example.com"}), 10).unwrap_err().code, "E_INPUT");
    61	    assert_eq!(device.predict("add", "alice", &json!({
    62	        "id":"bad", "category":"museum", "contact":"a..b@example.com", "amount":0
    63	    }), 10, vec![], 1).unwrap_err().code(), "E_CONSTRAINT", "{}", host.label());
    64	    let hits = device.query("search", "alice", &json!({"q":"cof","c":null}), 10).unwrap();
    65	    assert_eq!(hits.data.as_array().unwrap().len(), 1);
    66	    assert_eq!(hits.data.as_array().unwrap()[0].field("id").unwrap(), &id("a"));
    67	
    68	    let watermark = pages.last().unwrap().watermark;
    69	    let result = server.mutate("add", None, Context::new("alice", [
    70	        ("id".into(), id("c")), ("category".into(), Value::String("museum".into())),
    71	        ("contact".into(), Value::String("c@example.com".into())), ("amount".into(), Value::Int(9)),
    72	    ].into_iter().collect())).unwrap();
    73	    assert!(matches!(result.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{result:?}");
    74	    device.apply(&stream_page(&mut server, "alice", watermark), false).unwrap();
    75	    assert_eq!(device.query("totalEntries", "alice", &json!({}), 10).unwrap().data, Value::Int(3));
    76	    host.close("alice", device);
    77	    let mut reopened = host.open("alice", None).unwrap();
    78	    assert_eq!(reopened.query("totalEntries", "alice", &json!({}), 10).unwrap().data, Value::Int(3),
    79	        "the compiler-owned global discriminator survives store reopen");
    80	    host.close("alice", reopened);
    81	}
    82	
    83	#[test]
    84	fn wave12_semantics_match_on_both_device_stores() {
    85	    parity(&mut Memory::new());
    86	    parity(&mut Sqlite::new("wave12-language"));
    87	}

# Totals: count and sum in queries, mutations, jobs and views

Count rows or add up a column with `count` and `sum` inside a query, mutation or job. A view keeps a total per group current on every write, so reading it costs one row.

```quarry
table orders:
  owner: principal
  status: text <=10
  cents: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
view spentByOwner: sum orders of .cents by .owner
  online only
view spentPerDay: sum orders of .cents by .owner, day(.at)
  online only

query myTotals(since: time):
  open = count(orders where .owner = viewer and .status = 'open')
  recent = sum(orders where .owner = viewer and .at >= since, .cents)
  return { open, recent, lifetime: spentByOwner[viewer], today: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now)], yesterday: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now) - 1] }
```

There are two spellings, and they do not mix:

- **Inside an operation** (query, mutation, job): `count(t where …)` and
  `sum(t where …, .col)`. The parentheses are required; the column is the
  second argument, written `.col`. `min` and `max` take the same arguments.
- **In a view** (declared at top level): `view name: count t by .key` or
  `view name: sum t of .col by .key`, optionally `where …` before `by`
  (`sum t of .col where … by .key`, in that order). Read
  it by its group key, `spentByOwner[viewer]`; a group with no rows reads `0`.
  Omit `by` for one global scalar, `view orderCount: count orders`, and read it
  as `orderCount`. Its authorized empty value is `0` even though zero has no
  physical aggregate row.
  A view over an `online only` table is read online; its own `online only`
  line is optional.

## In a job

A job's totals are ordinary domain reads inside its sweep; write them where a
screen can read them:

```quarry
table readings:
  owner: principal
  value: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table sensors:
  owner: principal
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table dailyStats:
  owner: principal
  day: time
  n: int
  total: int
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- deny
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only

job dailyTotals(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):
  for s in sensors by .id:
    n = count(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd)
    total = sum(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd, .value)
    insert dailyStats { owner: s.owner, day: periodStart, n, total }
schedule nightly every 1d at 00:05: dailyTotals
```

`periodStart` and `periodEnd` are the schedule's period, so each run totals
one day. When the job only needs a number a screen shows, a view is usually
simpler than a job: `view perDay: sum readings of .value by .owner, day(.at)`
read as `perDay[viewer, day(now)]`.

## Rows the caller partly reads

An inline total counts only what its caller may read, so `check` refuses one
whose read rule its pins do not decide (`E_QUARRY_BOUND`, or
`E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT` in a mutation): it would be a different number for
each caller, and a capacity check would miss hidden rows. Three exact forms:
pin the deciding column (`.owner = viewer`: the caller's own total); or keep
the total in a view whose read rule names who sees it (the view discloses the
total, never the rows); or, in a mutation, `require` below a view's count.

```quarry
table staff:
  principal: principal
  role: text <=20
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  online only
table requests:
  owner: principal
  dept: text <=20
  amount: money USD
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
view requestsTotal: sum requests of .amount by .dept
  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
view requestsByOwner: sum requests of .amount by .owner, .dept
  read <- .owner = viewer

query deptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsTotal[dept] ! else NOT_ALLOWED
query myDeptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsByOwner[viewer, dept]
```

Finance reads every department's total; each requester reads their own.
`E_QUARRY_BOUND`'s rewrite prints this view for your table, column and group.
A keyed private view stays nullable until its own read rule is proved. For
example, after `require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
else NOT_FINANCE`, `requestsTotal[dept] + 1` is well typed and a readable
missing group contributes zero. A hidden stored group and a hidden miss both
remain null. A following write or viewer change invalidates the proof before
another keyed read.
A view may omit `by` for a whole-table scalar: `view requestsCount: count
requests`, read as bare `requestsCount`. The scalar still obeys its view read
rule: an authorized absence is zero, while a hidden stored or absent value is
null. Keep a real group key when callers need independent totals.
A guard on the read rule's own grant admits an inline total, as it admits a
staff page (`guide indexes`): after `require exists staff[principal = viewer,
role = 'finance'] else …` (or inside `if` on it), `sum(requests, .amount)` is
every row's total, because every caller who reaches it reads every row. A guard
on any other role does not; use the view.

For extrema over a domain, first establish that exact domain is non-empty:
`n = count(orders where .owner = viewer)` followed by `require n > 0 else
EMPTY`. From there, `min` and `max` of a required column on the identical
domain are required values; `sum` already has a zero identity. Optional projected columns remain optional. A
write, local rebinding, viewer/authority scope change, or different key/filter
invalidates the proof.

## Choosing

- A total a screen shows on every visit: a view. It is maintained on each
  write and read by key, constant work regardless of history.
- A rolling window (last 7 days): bucket the view by `day(.at)` (or
  `hour(.at)`, `bucket(.at, 15m)`) and add the buckets you need with point
  reads, `perDay[viewer, day(now)] + perDay[viewer, day(now) - 1]`.
- A one-off or filtered total (since an argument, by status): `count(...)` or
  `sum(...)` in the operation. It streams the domain under the meter: each
  row is authorized, filtered, folded and released. Its work grows with the
  matching rows, and `check --cost` says so; bound the predicate by an indexed
  time or a group key.
- `count(page)` and `sum(page, .col)` over a page you already read count only
  that page, not the population.

## Details

A meter cut never yields a partial total: the read is unavailable (the client
shows `loading`), including before `??`. On a device, an inline total needs
the device to hold its whole domain; otherwise the observation stays
`loading`, never a partial total. Sums take `int`, `decimal` or `money` columns; a total
that overflows refuses the transaction. `guide maintains` has every view form
(filters, reference paths, time buckets, offsets and sync); `guide reads`
covers totals beside pages and `exists`; `guide jobs` the sweep a job's totals
live in.

Common misspellings and what `check` says:

- `sum(orders of .cents)` (the view spelling inside an operation):
  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape of`. Write `sum(orders where …, .cents)`.
- `count orders where .owner = viewer` without parentheses:
  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape where`. Write `count(orders where …)`.
- `sum(orders where …, cents)`: `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown name cents`. The
  column is `.cents`.

Reference-grouped views over `read through .parent` rows inherit the parent's
rule. Omit a read line that repeats `.parent.owner = viewer`; it is
`E_REDUNDANT_RULE`, and deleting the whole line preserves the inherited audience.
Maintained sums spell the value separately: `view total: sum expenses of .amount by .household`,
not `sum expenses.amount by .household`.
# Views: plan, maintain or materialize

**Start here:** Use `view` for derived data: filters and unions plan reads, while counts update in the source transaction. For public post totals, use this excerpt from the complete example below; do not maintain counters in client writes.

```quarry
view postCount: count posts by .author
query total(author: principal):
  return postCount[author]
```

- `E_MAINTAIN_READ_RULE`: grouping lost an authorization field; retain it as a bare key or declare the aggregate's intended read rule.
- `E_QUARRY_SYNTAX`: a view cannot have its own sync line; keep inherited sync or choose `online only` and lose offline coverage.
- `N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED`: the aggregate does not inherit the source horizon; measure it, or choose `online only` only if offline reads and prediction by the named dependent mutations are unnecessary (`predicts` then fails with `E_NOT_PREDICTABLE`).

## Reference

```quarry
table posts:
  author: principal
  body: text <=500
  at: time
  removed: bool default false
  public 'public posts'
  online only
table reposts:
  actor: principal
  post: posts
  at: time
  public 'public reposts'
  online only
view activity:
  posts where not .removed { post: .id, actor: .author, at, repost: false }
  reposts { post, actor, at, repost: true }
query global(c: cursor ?):
  return activity last 20 by .at after c
view postCount: count posts by .author
query total(author: principal):
  return postCount[author]
```

A filter or projection is planned when read. Branches form a union without a copied application table. An aggregate is maintained in the source write's transaction. Public Following is materialized per reader when its source and edge rules admit that disclosure (`guide feeds`).

Each union branch runs under its own source's read rule before filtering or projection. Branches have compatible fields and types. Identity includes both branch and source id; equal ids in different tables remain distinct events. The exposed `id` is the source id. Include a branch discriminator, such as `repost`, when forming a client list key. A predicate on a projected column reaches each source; a constant predicate selects branches. Order fields must map to source columns in every branch.

A maintained view has rows `{ id, <group columns>, value }`. A full group key returns a scalar. An authorized miss is zero; a hidden hit and hidden miss are both null. Omit `by` for one global scalar such as `view totalPosts: count posts`, read it by its bare name, and receive zero for authorized absence; its compiler-owned physical group key is not an application column. Pages return existing groups, using `first`/`last`, `by .column`
and `after c`. A domain may use an inherited product cap or the runtime meter.
Source caps can bound distinct groups; no new target cap is enforced.

During adoption, query and sync snapshot reads keep reading the previous backend. A newly added query answers pending until the views are complete. Writes wait, then execute once.
`check` reports N_ADOPTION_PAUSE; dev and deploy print it before activating. Plan the write pause before adopting. Source counts estimate work at measured eb1000 rates; the subtotal is not a floor. The modeled “up to” time is not an SLO or hard bound. Removed or rebuilt rows/indexes add an explicitly unestimated retirement pause, including tables whose names survive. Tables affected by read-through or inherited-expiry changes warn about retirement/recapture: the deletion subset depends on activation time, so their stored populations are not exact deletion counts. On a schema change, an unaffected read-through table separately rewrites every stored row to recompute deadlines; this rewrite count is exact, not a deletion subset. Both pauses remain unestimated. Maintained-column mirrors, cap resets and asset-home retirement also warn when unpriced. Payload width, fanout, cache and WAL/storage affect time.
Activation defaults to a 64 MiB combined main/temp cache target (half each). Set SNAPBACK4_ADOPTION_CACHE_MIB to 0–512; 0 keeps caller settings and larger caller caches are retained. Linux reduces the target for address-space headroom. Workspace is not an RSS cap: the prior 64 MiB main-cache run used about 147 MiB process RSS; sorter RAM follows the cache. SQLite NOMEM after enlargement rolls back and retries once at caller settings; choose 0 for constrained hosts.
The adoption line reports phases, capture fills and rebuilt views with source visits and milliseconds; nested details are included in their parent phase. A failed or interrupted attempt keeps the previous backend; restart retries the complete build.
Signed-in `GET /changes` waits for the whole adoption because reconnecting
requires a durable presence write. That wait grows with the store; it is not
bounded by a rebuild chunk. Query and sync reads have reserved admission capacity
so queued mutations cannot occupy all their request slots.

Omit `read` to derive readability: a public/allow source gives `read <- allow`;
otherwise every field in its read rule must survive as a bare group key, and
that same rule protects the aggregate. Calendar keys and reference paths do
not preserve rule fields. E_MAINTAIN_READ_RULE names missing fields; state an
explicit `read <-` when the aggregate needs its own audience. An explicit
rule always wins. Residency is unchanged: sync is inherited only when all
source audience columns survive as bare keys, without the source horizon;
otherwise the aggregate stays server-only. `online only` opts out.

E_SYNC on a public inherited view names the source, the missing horizon and
the conflicting explicit read. An owner-only read does not make public sync
private. E_QUARRY_SYNTAX refuses a separate sync line on any view. On this public inherited view, remove that
line, then keep the public read (or omit it) for the public offline summary,
or use `online only` and lose offline aggregate/absence coverage. Never narrow
the public source to make the view private. `guide offline-interactions` is
the example and replica census; N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED links it too. For viewer-owned
existence on an eligible public window, that card shows source owner-key
coverage with ordinary `exists` instead of a summary; it does not supply totals.

E_MAINTAIN_READ_EXPIRE means an aggregate over an expiring source has a read
rule that uses fields its group keys do not determine. This includes a source
with an effective deadline inherited through `read through`. Use `read <- deny`
and replace deny with a rule over the group keys, omit `read` to inherit the
source rule, or use `online only` with an explicit key rule. Neither a source
cap nor `online only` permits a value-dependent rule. Non-expiring sources
may authorize their stored value because it is already the live value.

A predicate accepts scalar comparisons, and/or/not, boolean literals and bare
required boolean columns. Equality permits null; ordering requires compatible
non-optional scalars. It cannot read tables, viewer, now, helpers or arithmetic:
store that fact on the source, or use a bounded job. False rows contribute
nothing; updates subtract and add once, coalescing equal groups. Literal
defaults mean the same thing on writes, devices, expiry and adoption.

day(.at), hour(.at) and bucket(.at, 2m) take required time columns.
They also accept an admitted reference path, such as hour(postId.at); a null
recorded time produces the null group. Their int
outputs are floor ordinals from the Unix UTC epoch; day(-1 ms) is -1.
Computed fields are named day, hour and bucket, once each; id and value are
reserved. The same functions work in queries as day(now), hour(now) and
bucket(now, 2m).
The same three functions are table index components over required time columns
at any key position; see `guide indexes` for listings and daily uniqueness.
An optional literal offset anchors the boundary: day(.at, 8h) or
bucket(.at, 1d, 8h) groups 08:00 UTC through just before the next 08:00.
The ordinal is floor((at - offset) / period); offsets are nonnegative whole
durations smaller than the period, including 0ms. hour(.at, 30m) works too.
The same offset belongs in scalar lookups: day(now, 8h). Days remain exactly
86,400,000 ms; no timezone or daylight-saving adjustment is applied. Changing the offset
rebuilds the view without adding a source column.

The following Clique summaries are online-only. Add source sync when its
audience is invertible, and omit the view's online only to inherit it
when every audience column survives as a bare key.

```quarry
table posts:
  authorId: principal

     3	use crate::ast::{ArgDecl, Expr, FunctionBody, FunctionDecl, Literal, ReadExpr};

     6	// @ref LLP 3000.013#5.1 — choose an access path, leaving aggregate scan
     7	// admission and streaming to the consumer's existing binder/runtime contract.
     8	#[derive(Clone, Default)]
     9	pub(super) struct Contexts {
    10	    pub domains: BTreeSet<String>,
    11	    pub extrema: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    12	    required: BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>>,
    13	}
    14	impl Contexts {
    15	    pub fn safe(&self, read: &ReadExpr, value: &Expr) -> bool {
    16	        safe_endpoint(value, self.required.get(&read.site.to_string()).unwrap_or(&BTreeSet::new()))
    17	    }
    18	    fn visit(&mut self, value: &mut Expr, tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, functions: &BTreeMap<String, FunctionDecl>, required: &BTreeSet<String>) {
    19	        if let Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } = value {
    20	            self.required.insert(read.site.to_string(), required.clone());
    21	        }
    22	        if let Expr::Call { name, args, .. } = value
    23	            && is_consumer(name, args, tables) && !functions.contains_key(name)
    24	            && let Some(Expr::Read(read)) = args.first() && read.range.is_none() {
    25	            self.domains.insert(read.site.to_string());
    26	            if matches!(name.as_str(), "min" | "max") && let Some(field) = args.get(1).and_then(intent::column) {
    27	                self.extrema.insert(read.site.to_string(), field.into());
    28	            }
    29	        }
    30	        consumer(value, tables, functions, required);
    31	    }
    32	}
    33	pub(super) fn order(catalog: &mut Catalog) -> Contexts {
    34	    let mut contexts = Contexts::default();
    35	    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
    36	        for program in &mut doc.programs {
    37	            let required = required_args(&program.args);
    38	            let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| contexts.visit(value, &catalog.tables, &catalog.functions, &required);
    39	            intent_walk::statements(&mut program.prelude, &mut visit);
    40	            intent_walk::statements(&mut program.body, &mut visit);
    41	        }
    42	        for function in &mut doc.functions {
    43	            let required = required_args(&function.args);
    44	            let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| contexts.visit(value, &catalog.tables, &catalog.functions, &required);
    45	            match &mut function.body {
    46	                FunctionBody::Expression(value) => intent_walk::expression(value, &mut visit),
    47	                FunctionBody::Block(body) => intent_walk::statements(body, &mut visit),
    48	            }
    49	        }
    50	        for shape in &mut doc.shapes {
    51	            intent_walk::shape(&mut shape.fields, &mut |value| contexts.visit(value, &catalog.tables, &catalog.functions, &BTreeSet::new()));
    52	        }
    53	    }
    54	    contexts
    55	}

    58	// Optional filters and inferred lanes may materialize an ordinary read before

    60	// a binary collection comparison still receives its original page plan.
    61	pub(super) fn remember_domains(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    62	    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
    63	        intent_walk::document(doc, &mut |expr| {
    64	            if let Expr::Call { name, args, .. } = expr
    65	                && matches!(name.as_str(), "min" | "max") && args.len() == 2
    66	                && !catalog.functions.contains_key(name) && intent::column(&args[1]).is_none()
    67	                && let Expr::Read(read) = &args[0]
    68	                && read.range.is_none() && read.matching.is_none()
    69	                && catalog.tables.get(&read.table).is_some_and(|table|
    70	                    !table.maintained.as_ref().is_some_and(|maintain| maintain.by.is_empty())) {

    77	fn required_args(args: &[ArgDecl]) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    78	    args.iter().filter(|arg| !arg.type_.optional).map(|arg| arg.name.clone()).collect()
    79	}
    80	
    81	pub(super) fn is_consumer(name: &str, args: &[Expr], tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>) -> bool {
    82	    if matches!(name, "count" | "sum") { return true; }
    83	    if !matches!(name, "min" | "max") { return false; }
    84	    let field = args.get(1).and_then(intent::column);
    85	    // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.2 — expression extrema use ordinary aggregate scan planning.
    86	    // Collection-valued second arguments keep binary page comparison planning.
    87	    if field.is_none() && args.get(1).is_none_or(may_be_collection) { return false; }
    88	    // A binary comparison of two pages keeps their observable row order.
    89	    // A focal member absent from this table can belong to an outer shape.
    90	    match args.first() {
    91	        Some(Expr::Read(read)) => tables.get(&read.table).is_some_and(|table|
    92	            extremum_domain(read, table) && field.is_none_or(|field| field == "id" || table.columns.contains_key(field))),
    93	        _ => true,
    94	    }
    95	}
    96	
    97	// Before type binding, only select an aggregate order for a definitely scalar
    98	// result. Otherwise leave the source order intact for the binary overload;
    99	// the binder can still admit a scalar aggregate over that ordinary scan.
   100	fn may_be_collection(value: &Expr) -> bool {
   101	    match value {
   102	        Expr::Literal { .. } | Expr::Unary { .. } | Expr::Exists { .. } | Expr::Created { .. } => false,
   103	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "??" || op == "+" => may_be_collection(left) || may_be_collection(right),
   104	        Expr::Binary { .. } => false,
   105	        Expr::If { then, otherwise, .. } => may_be_collection(then) || may_be_collection(otherwise),
   106	        Expr::Assert { value, .. } => may_be_collection(value),
   107	        // Calls can be user helpers, including helpers which return pages.
   108	        _ => true,
   109	    }
   110	}

   451	            },
   452	            // A scalar already *is* the value, so the repair is to drop the field access,
   453	            // not to go and find a row. `int has no fields` was the most frequent compile
   454	            // error of the 0.2.28 calibration — 20 of its 32 E_QUARRY_TYPE — and the
   455	            // generic advice pointed away from the fix: a full group-key `maintain`
   456	            // lookup is its count, and the author had written `.value` on it.
   457	            // `Ty::Id` keeps the old advice: an id is exactly the case where a row read
   458	            // is what is missing.
   459	            other @ (Ty::Bool | Ty::Int | Ty::Time | Ty::Decimal(_) | Ty::Money(..) | Ty::Text(_) | Ty::Principal | Ty::Enum(_)) => {
   460	                return Err(type_error(site, format!("{} has no fields", other.name()), format!("this is already a value — remove `.{name}`")));
   461	            }
   462	            other => return Err(type_error(site, format!("{} has no fields", other.name()), "read a row or an object before selecting a field")),
   463	        };
   464	        let result_ty = if ty.is_optional() { field_ty.optional() } else { field_ty };
   465	        Ok((ir::field(base, name), result_ty))
   466	    }

   468	    pub(super) fn binary(&mut self, op: &str, left: &A, right: &A, site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
   469	        if op == "in" {
   470	            let (value, ty) = self.expr(left, out)?;
   471	            let value = self.snapshot(value, out);
   472	            let (domain, item) = self.loop_domain(right, out)?;
   473	            if !ty.comparable(&item) { return Err(type_error(site, format!("in compares {} with {}", ty.name(), item.name()), "use a list of values of the column's type")); }
   474	            let found = self.temp("contains");
   475	            let binding = self.temp("member");
   476	            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: found.clone(), value: ir::lit(Value::Bool(false)) });
   477	            let body = vec![Stmt::If { condition: Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(value), right: Box::new(ir::var(&binding)) },
   478	                then_body: vec![Stmt::Assign { name: found.clone(), value: ir::lit(Value::Bool(true)) }, Stmt::Break], else_body: Vec::new() }];
   479	            out.push(Stmt::For { binding, domain, body });
   480	            return Ok((ir::var(&found), Ty::Bool));
   481	        }
   482	        if op == "??" {
   483	            let (l, lt) = self.expr(left, out)?;
   484	            let tmp = self.temp("or");
   485	            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: l });
   486	            let mut fallback = setup::Body::default();
   487	            // `conversationId ?? 'dm1'`: a text literal after an identity is
   488	            // an id, as it is beside `=` and in a key.
   489	            let (r, rt) = if lt.required().is_identity() { self.expr_as_identity(right, &mut fallback, &lt.required())? } else { self.expr(right, &mut fallback)? };
   490	            fallback.push(Stmt::Assign { name: tmp.clone(), value: r });
   491	            out.push_if(Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(ir::var(&tmp)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) }, fallback, setup::Body::default());
   492	            let joined = self.join(&lt.required(), &rt, left, right)?;
   493	            let ty = if rt.is_optional() { joined } else { joined.required() };
   494	            return Ok((ir::var(&tmp), ty));
   495	        }
   496	        let (l, lt) = self.expr(left, out)?;
   497	        if op == "and" || op == "or" {
   498	            let result = self.temp("bool");
   499	            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: result.clone(), value: l });
   500	            let mut branch = setup::Body::default();
   501	            self.push_scope();
   502	            self.narrow(left, op == "and");
   503	            let right = self.expr(right, &mut branch);
   504	            self.pop_scope();
   505	            let (r, rt) = right?;
   506	            if !lt.is_bool() || !rt.is_bool() {
   507	                return Err(type_error(site, format!("{op} needs bool operands; got {} and {}", lt.name(), rt.name()), "compare each side first"));
   508	            }
   509	            branch.push(Stmt::Assign { name: result.clone(), value: r });
   510	            let (then_body, else_body) = if op == "and" { (branch, setup::Body::default()) } else { (setup::Body::default(), branch) };
   511	            out.push_if(ir::var(&result), then_body, else_body);
   512	            return Ok((ir::var(&result), Ty::Bool));
   513	        }
   514	        let coerce_right = lt.is_identity();
   515	        let mut right_setup = setup::Body::default();
   516	        let (r, rt) = if coerce_right { self.expr_as_identity(right, &mut right_setup, &lt)? } else { self.expr(right, &mut right_setup)? };
   517	        let (l, lt) = if rt.is_identity() && !lt.is_identity() {
   518	            match (left, &l) {
   519	                (A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(text), .. }, _) => (Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())), Ty::Id),

18-    18	Reference contexts project required rows’ `.id`. Media: decimal KB/MB/GB.
19-    19	
20-    20	`?` makes columns/arguments nullable; omission is null. Table references do not
21-    21	read targets: use `require exists t[id] else CODE` or
22-    22	`r = t[id] ! else CODE`.
23:    23	Defaults fill omissions; explicit wins. Formats: handle/url/email; identity may
24:    24	be stricter than the email contract below. Money/decimal are
25:    25	scaled integers; fractions refuse E_INPUT. `divide` names rounding and int/int
26-    26	result scale. Unique-read/positive-count guards narrow; `sum` has zero. Writes,
27-    27	rebinding or viewer changes invalidate.
28-    28	
29-    29	## Limits
30-    30	
31-    31	- Guard or assert nullable values before passing them to required parameters: E_QUARRY_TYPE.
32-    32	- Values and arithmetic must fit the integer range: E_OVERFLOW.
33-    33	- `/` and `%` take ints and floor: `-7 / 2` is -4, `-7 % 2` is 1. A divisor must be nonzero: E_DIV_ZERO.
34:    34	- `divide(value, int, mode)` preserves kind; modes are `toward-zero`,
35:    35	  `away-from-zero`, `floor`, `ceiling`, `half-away-from-zero`, `half-even`.
36-    36	  Zero: E_DIV_ZERO; overflow: E_OVERFLOW.
37:    37	- `divide(int, int, scale, mode)` returns `decimal scale` (literal 0–12) with
38-    38	  checked wide scaling and an i53 result. Null is not zero.
39-    39	- New required defaults must also fit the existing store: E_ADOPT.
40-    40	
41-    41	## Details
42-    42	
43-    43	Types describe the values a column or argument accepts.
44-    44	They also set limits on text, lists and uploaded assets.

107-   107	
108-   108	`text` defaults to `text <=10000` characters in columns, arguments and helper
109-   109	parameters. Write `text <=N` or `text M..N` when the bound is a product rule;
110-   110	an indexed column must also fit the key budget (`guide indexes`).
111-   111	
112:   112	`format email` accepts one ASCII `@`. Its 1–64 character local part uses
113-   113	letter/digit/`!#$%&'*+-/=?^_`{|}~` dot-separated atoms; its 1–253 character
114-   114	domain uses 1–63 character letter/digit/hyphen labels. Dots cannot bound/repeat;
115-   115	hyphens cannot bound labels. `alice@localhost` is valid. Case/Unicode are not
116-   116	normalized; store a normalized lookup when needed.
